# Roadmap

The living work board of the repository. It answers: what to do, in what order, based on which
design, what counts as done, and where each item stands. This page **does not** change the
design.

Last updated: 2026-11-21 (F10.20 split into F10.20a host app-state store and F10.20b app slot grid; F10.20a in review).

## 0. Context

- **Product:** Fugue by SynX. This repository is the **all-in-one Go core**: one host process
  (`fugue serve`) owns the data directory, the vault, the store and every service; the desktop
  app (Gio, `desktop-app`) and other clients talk to it through the Host API.
- **DevKit (Wails)** is frozen at its current release and no longer developed. This repository
  **does not** import or depend on `syurodev/dev-kit`.
- **Goal of this phase (since 2026-11-07):** the **vendor runtime** (stage A of `activation`
  v0.14): Fugue drives the first-party CLIs (Claude Code, Codex `app-server`, Antigravity `acp`)
  under the user's own logins, shows them through one transcript model in a Gio desktop app, and
  lets a session propose a multi-level team of agents on different vendors that the user
  approves. The design pages are `vendor-runtime` (H-47), `transcript` (H-48), `orchestration`
  (H-49) and `desktop-app` (H-33). The native harness (kernel, store, coordinator, model
  adapters, tools) is **stage B**; what is already built stays and is shared (storage, vault,
  crypto, codec, Host API, interaction, timers).
- **Design** lives in the base-doc repository under `content/projects/dev-kit/core-harness/`.
  The tables below cite pages by name, for example `state-machine §3`. The design was written
  for DevKit, so its path names (`internal/...`, `devkit.db`, `AppService`) are superseded by
  this repository's ADRs; semantics stay the same. Real semantic differences are listed in
  [§7](#7-needs-decision).
- **No harness code is carried over** from dev-kit (`internal/harnessruntime`,
  `internal/execstore`, `internal/hostapi` from PRs #412 to #420). The harness is written from
  scratch.
- **Data starts clean:** data directory `os.UserConfigDir()/Fugue/`, new schema. Importing
  DevKit data is a separate, later item.
- **Stage A** is defined by `activation` v0.14 (2026-11-07, supersedes v0.13 of Q-02): vendor
  runtime, the owner dogfoods it, Linux and macOS, the Gio desktop app as the only client (the
  CLI/TUI client is stage B since 2026-11-08; the binary keeps `fugue serve` and `fugue
  mcp-bridge`), one release gate. Native runs, API providers (OpenRouter first, M-12) and Sign in with ChatGPT move to
  stage B. Items with status `Deferred` or marked *Stage B* in their notes do not start before
  stage A ships.

## 1. Rules for the implementing agent

1. **Read before working:** `AGENTS.md`, [quality gates](quality-gates.md), the relevant ADRs in
   `docs/adr/`, and exactly the design pages the item points to.
2. **Work only on items whose design is settled.** Items in `Awaiting design` do not start.
3. **Never change design or gates on your own.** When something is wrong, missing or
   contradictory, move the item to `Needs decision`, record the question in
   [§7](#7-needs-decision), and take another unblocked item.
4. **Roadmap work: one item, one branch, one PR** into `main`, branch `<type>/<id>-<slug>` (for
   example `feat/f2.1-kernel-types`). A change outside the roadmap (bug, chore, docs) is also
   allowed as its own branch and PR, without an item ID. Never stack a PR on an unmerged PR.
   Keep PRs under roughly 600 changed lines, excluding generated code, golden files and
   testdata.
5. **Done** means: merged into `main`, every CI gate green, and the tests in the Evidence column
   exist and pass. `TestRoadmapEvidence` checks this.
6. **Update this page in the same PR:** status, PR link, evidence test names, one log line.
7. **Port from DevKit** under [§3](#3-porting-rules).

**Statuses:** `Not started` · `In progress` · `In review` · `Done` · `Blocked` (name the
blocking ID) · `Needs decision` · `Awaiting design` · `Deferred` (moved to stage B; does not
start) · `Dropped` (replaced; the note names what replaces it).

## 2. Phase overview

| Phase | Goal | Exit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| F0 | Repository foundation: names, layout, every quality gate, CI, platform primitives | `task check` green on a nearly empty repository; every gate has a negative test |
| F1 | Port dependencies: crypto, vault, storage and data-directory lock, credentials, interaction, sandbox, snapshot | Each package passes the same gates as new code; vector tests prove behavior is preserved |
| F2 | Headless vertical slice (F2.10 is *Stage B*): kernel, codec, execution store, coordinator, faux model, fake read tool, continuation | Trace A reproduced exactly; replay calls neither model nor tool |
| F3 | Recovery, retry, timers, budgets and fault injection (only F3.6 stays in stage A) | Traces B and C; every row of the validation criteria has a test |
| F4 | *Stage B*: context engine and model adapters | Evidence tables of the context engine and model adapter §11 |
| F5 | Host API (`fugue serve`), input coordination and workspaces | Evidence tables of the Host API and input coordination; `workspace §9` |
| F6 | Tools and interaction (stage A keeps F6.3 and F6.7a) | Evidence of `interaction §11`, `sandbox-runtime §12`, `shell-job §8`, `content-safety §7`, `file-tools §11`, `ask-plan §7`, `tasks §12` |
| F7 | *Stage B*: CLI/TUI client (dropped from stage A on 2026-11-08) | Evidence of `cli §9` |
| F8 | Stage A release | Evidence of `activation §5` (v0.14) |
| F9 | Vendor runtime: process supervisor, vendor store, transcript, Claude, Codex and agy adapters, approvals, session service, accounts | Evidence of `vendor-runtime §15` and `transcript §9` |
| F10 | Gio desktop app: IME go/no-go, client package, markdown, transcript and composer, approvals, accounts, vault | Evidence of `desktop-app §10` (DA-E01–DA-E06) |
| F11 | Teams: proposal and approval, multi-level delegation, routing, git, result gates, layered integration, scheduler, quota, multiple accounts | Evidence of `orchestration §15`, DA-E07 |

## 3. Porting rules

Applies to all code copied from `syurodev/dev-kit`.

1. Copy **only what is needed**, one small package at a time; one PR per package.
2. Record the source in `docs/ports.md`: original path, dev-kit commit SHA, what was dropped or
   changed.
3. Remove every DevKit dependency: Wails, `bridge`, `app`, `AppService`, `DEVKIT_*` variables,
   the DevKit name.
4. Ported code passes **exactly the same gates as new code**: lint, coverage, mutation, `nolint`
   ratchet. Never add `nolint` to get a port through.
5. Preserve behavior with **vector tests**: before changing anything, write tests that capture
   the original code's output (for example ciphertext for a fixed key and nonce, a generated
   sandbox profile); those tests must still pass after the port.
6. Any change in behavior or format needs an ADR.
7. `crypto`, `vault` and `sandbox` PRs need the user's approval.

| F3.1 | Restart classification: Queued, `ToolCheckReset`, dispatching becomes unknown, vault locked, payload lost | `state-machine §8`, `event-storage-recovery §7` | F2.10 | Trace B; an unknown effect never reruns | Deferred |  | Recovery clears `recovery_admission` automatically when committed facts prove quiescence: no model attempt Requesting or Streaming and no tool Dispatching; no open time window; no unknown; no `shutdown` suspension; and every remaining wait reason is in {`quota_refresh`, `budget(kind)`, `retry_timer`}; after recovering a session, call `Scheduler.Wake(sessionID)` so timers parked on ErrRecoveryRequired resume. *Stage B* since the vendor runtime pivot (activation v0.14, 2026-11-07) |
| F3.2 | Stop intent, cancel, quiesce, `AcceptUnknown`, `CloseRunWithoutPayload`, `SelectRunContext` | `state-machine §6`, `event-storage-recovery §3` | F3.1 | Trace C | Deferred |  | *Stage B* since the vendor runtime pivot (activation v0.14, 2026-11-07) |
| F3.3 | Model retry: `Planned` attempt, retry intent and timer written in one batch before waiting; timer elapsed only opens dispatch | `event-catalog §3`, `model-adapter §3` | F2.10 | A crash while waiting creates no new attempt and charges nothing again; a stop while waiting cancels the attempt and the timer | Deferred |  | A usage-limit 429 uses failure class `quota_exhausted`, which immediately plans a new attempt on another account with no timer and no retry budget, or plans the attempt plus `quota_refresh` when no account is left. *Stage B* since the vendor runtime pivot (activation v0.14, 2026-11-07) |

Design: `vendor-runtime`, `transcript`, `host-api §5.1` (v0.14). Order: F9.1, then F9.2–F9.4,
then the Claude path (F9.5, F9.6, F9.9) before Codex and agy.

Starts after F9.6a merges; its PR marks F9.6a Done. The adapter delivers each relayed decision through `interaction.Owner.TakeRelayed`, which returns the resolved ticket once and frees its content. Q-87 A (`interaction` v0.16): relayed `KindConfirm` carries full compact JSON input in `Detail` (up to `MaxRelayedDetailBytes` = 256 KiB, identical bytes to `updatedInput`), `Summary` stays a <= 2 KiB header with explicit truncation marker, and > 256 KiB creates no request and returns the generic tool error (`SessionToolMaxBytes` = 512 KiB). The vault-locked behavior stays fail closed (I-E06). Live VR-E04 checklist (owner, real Claude account, not part of the PR): (1) a denied and an allowed tool call each reach the app verbatim and Claude receives the choice; (2) the `"env": {"FUGUE_SESSION_TOKEN": "${FUGUE_SESSION_TOKEN}"}` entry of `--mcp-config` expands in Claude Code, so the bridge attaches (F9.6a delivers the token this way and does not rely on Claude passing its own environment on); (3) with the variable missing or empty the bridge exits with a clear typed error and Claude shows the server as failed, never an unauthenticated one. |

One PR over the ceiling (Q-90, decided by the owner, 2026-11-14). Pinned schema and real-CLI fixtures recorded from `codex-cli 0.167.0` and redacted; slash commands ported from DevKit `internal/agenthub/codex_commands.go` (`docs/ports.md`) keeping `/compact` (`thread/compact/start`) and `/review` (`review/start`) measured working on 0.167.0. Fugue never answers a Codex approval or user-input request: detail over 256 KiB, drain, vault lock, session close, revoked/ended session, and expiry all return a JSON-RPC error response, never a Fugue-made accept or decline; retried question tombstones store the decision itself (`allow`, `selectedOption`, `answers`) without labels and replay identical JSON-RPC bytes; secret questions (`isSecret: true`) fail closed with a JSON-RPC error and an unsupported notice (Q-92); command approvals with `proposedExecpolicyAmendment` relay and encode `acceptWithExecpolicyAmendment`. VR-E04 with the real account stays a live record for the owner. Touches ADR 0001 and ADR 0002 (CODEOWNERS review). |

Split per Q-94 B (depends on F9.8a; 990 gross non-test Go lines). Shares `RelayServerRequest`, `awaitAndTakeCodexRelayed`, and `approveTombstones` in `internal/app/session_codex_relay.go` and `session_approve.go` with Codex (`putAgy` stores only the outcome `selectedOption` or `cancelled`, `allow` derived from option `kind`, and digest without labels, replaying byte-identically on both tombstone replay and `TakeRelayed`-not-found fallback). On user interrupt, `CancelTurn` closes pending tickets with `OutcomeCancelled` and `TurnCancelled: true` and remembers the cancelled `(sessionID, turnID)` (bounded to 1,024 sessions, cleared on next turn or `ResetSession`) so late requests for that turn are answered immediately without a ticket: agy receives `{"outcome":"cancelled"}`, Codex receives a JSON-RPC `-32000` error, and Claude's MCP `approve` tool returns a tool error (`approveToolError()`). `agy.Session` bounds `seenApprovals` and `resolvedApprovals` to 256-entry ring windows. `available_commands_update` filters `fugue:`, empty, whitespace/control-char, and >64-byte names (capped at 128 commands), hiding `compact` and the 49 TUI-only commands from `agy 1.5.9`'s 13 advertised commands to expose 12 via `ListCommands`. VR-E04 (agy) with the real account stays a live record for the owner. |

The proto package is `fugue.vendors.v1`, not the module name `vendor` of `host-api §5.1`: Buf writes Go code under a path that mirrors the proto directory, and the Go toolchain treats a directory named `vendor` as vendored code (the Q-37 problem). The topic sequence is a new cross-session journal, migration `0009_vendor_session_changes.sql` (AUTOINCREMENT, so a purge never regresses a cursor). A session records its workspace ID and resolved root at Start (migration 0009 columns) and Resume reuses them; a client naming another workspace or root gets `vendor.workspace_mismatch` before spawn. The `/fugue:` rule ignores leading Unicode whitespace. The working directory comes only from `SelectSessionCwd`; the workspace re-validates roots on load, so the Q-57 rejections surface as `workspace.path_denied`. Fixes an F9.9b race: the actor ignores observations and vault events until the create or resume command commits, because a real process reports its init before the session row exists. The host vault stays locked until the unlock RPC of F10.5, so StartSession fails with `vendor.vault_locked` on a real host until then; startup `Recover` and the resume-over-socket E2E stay with F9.9e. |

Recovery runs inside StartHost (Recover, then Reap of the recorded identities) before the Host API accepts a client: unfinished sessions come back detached, open turns unknown, no respawn or replay. Stop order and its recorded stop proof unchanged. Supervisor wiring (F9.2b review notes): raw `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` from the injected environment, one `SystemdProbe` per vendor runtime, scope unit derived only from the SessionID (a respawn fails closed while the old scope has processes), SessionID required typed before the probe. §13 defaults documented in `internal/process` doc.go; each limit fails typed (frame_too_large, deadline_exceeded, vendor.capacity). Change journal keeps its newest `ChangeJournalRetention` rows, pruned in the insert's transaction; a catch-up older than the oldest kept row fails typed and the topic falls back to a snapshot or ends live subscribers with resync_required. The probe cache key fix arrives with F9.6a; rebase-verified. |

First half of the F9.10 split. Measured 823 gross non-test lines (785 additions, 38 deletions; excluding `pkg/pb`, `gen/ts`, docs, tests and testdata). Account state is held in memory in stage A: a host restart forgets a pause until the next quota observation or failed turn, and a pause that reached its reset time reads as `ok` at the next read without an event (clients use `paused_until`). The paused and failed(auth, quota) cases replay a recorded line with one field changed, because a real exhausted or logged-out account cannot be recorded on demand. |

Second half of the F9.10 split. Q-112 decided 2026-11-18: agy answers a typed FailedPrecondition ("agy has no login command; sign in from the agy app"); claude runs the default `claude auth login` flow with the URL relayed verbatim and ends `needs_terminal` at the timeout (the CLI finishes by reading a pasted code from its own stdin, which Fugue does not relay — a paste relay would belong with the F10.15 secret-answer mechanism); codex is `--device-auth` only (the default browser flow is never run again, not even for measurement — it once auto-completed a real login through the user's browser into a throwaway HOME); timeout 15 minutes, the codex device-code expiry. The login process runs through the F9.2 process supervisor (own process group, §8 stop order); progress streams on the new `login` topic with the CLI's printed lines relayed verbatim (intermediate states latest-for-key, final outcomes never dropped); a login counts as successful only after the F9.10a status probe says logged in. Also removed the test-only `refreshFlight.waiters` counter left over from F9.10a review; `TestConcurrentListAccountsShareOneRefresh` observes the single-flight behaviour without it. |
| F9.11 | Fugue MCP for Codex and agy: wire `fugue mcp-bridge` into Codex and agy sessions with the session principal token | `vendor-runtime §5.2, §5.3, §6, §8, §12`; VR-05, VR-13; `host-api §7` | F9.6, F9.7, F9.8 | `TestCodexSessionGetsTheFugueBridgeWithoutTheTokenInArgv`, `TestAgySessionNewCarriesTheFugueBridge`, `TestBridgeTokenNeverInArgvFilesOrLogs`, `TestFugueToolFromCodexSessionGoesThroughDecide`, `TestFugueToolFromAgySessionGoesThroughDecide`, `TestBridgeRefusesAnotherSessionsToken`, `TestVendorApprovalsStayRelayedWithTheBridgeAttached`, `TestCodexAndAgyFugueToolCallsNormalizeLikeClaude`, `TestToolListFollowsVendorAndTeamsFlag`; fixtures: `TestVRE02CodexFixturesReplayToGoldenTranscript`, `TestVRE03AgyFixturesReplayToGoldenTranscript` (new `*-fugue-tool` fixtures) | Done | [#151](https://github.com/syurodev/fugue/pull/151) | Merged `b5d7200`. Codex receives the bridge only as `-c mcp_servers.fugue.*` overrides on argv (command, args, `env_vars=["FUGUE_SESSION_TOKEN"]`, `enabled=true`): the `env_vars` pass-through copies the named variable from the codex process environment into the bridge child (measured on 0.167.0, 2026-11-16), so no file is written and the token is in neither argv nor any file. agy receives it as the `mcpServers` entry of `session/new`/`session/load` with no env pairs: agy children inherit the agy process environment (measured on 1.5.9, 2026-11-16), so no token value crosses the ACP pipe. Recorded real-CLI check (owner measurement, 2026-11-16): session + `ps -o args` and a grep of the codex home and temp dirs, and of `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli` and `~/Library/Application Support/antigravity`, found the token nowhere. The approve relay stays Claude-only: Codex and agy approvals arrive as server requests, so their bridge lists no approve tool (VR-05). Codex gates an MCP tool call with the server request `mcpServer/elicitation/request` (measured 0.167.0, 2026-11-16; absent from the F9.7 fixture set), relayed verbatim — only when it is the tool-call gate (form mode, `_meta.codex_approval_kind: "mcp_tool_call"`, empty requested schema); every other elicitation goes through the unsupported path (notice + JSON-RPC error response, VR-05). The gate offers the persistence scopes its `_meta.persist` lists, and the answer carries the measured response `_meta` (`persist: "session"` remembered for the session, `persist: "always"` persists across sessions — measured live on 0.167.0: one gate answered accept-for-session, second tool call ran with no second gate; confirmed against codex-rs `parse_mcp_tool_approval_elicitation_response` at rust-v0.167.0). The Codex fixture `codex-0.167.0-session4-fugue-tool` is a redacted real-CLI recording of a Fugue tool call through the real bridge; the agy fixture `agy-1.5.10-session7-fugue-tool` is a redacted real-CLI recording of two `fugue_test_echo` calls through the real bridge (recorder: `go test -tags fugue_record`, `FUGUE_RECORD_AGY=1`, model chosen with `FUGUE_RECORD_AGY_MODEL`, default `gemini-4-hydrogen`, selected through ACP `session/set_config_option`; see the agy RECORDING.md). It is recorded from agy 1.5.10, the version installed on 2026-11-17, not 1.5.9. Tool-list per vendor and teams flag: `TestToolListFollowsVendorAndTeamsFlag`. Finding, not fixed here: agy 1.5.10 rejects the flat `session/request_permission` answer `{"outcome":"selected",...}` that the adapter sends (the call then fails without reaching the bridge) and needs the nested `{"outcome":{"outcome":"selected","optionId":...}}`; the adapter is outside the tested range (1.5.9) and answers the flat form, so approvals on 1.5.10 need a separate fix. |
### F10 — Desktop app
Design: `desktop-app` (v0.5). F10.1 decides whether Gio stays; nothing else in F10 starts before
it is Done. The shared component kit follows `ui-kit` (H-51, v0.3): kit v1 is F10.8–F10.13 (spike
results in its §9; per Q-91 F10.8–F10.11 come before the composer F10.4b), kit v2 is
F10.16–F10.19. The app shell's slot grid (desktop-app v0.5 §5.3) is F10.20, many windows F10.21.

The editor core reports `Changed`/`Submitted`/`FocusLost` in the frame and its submit key belongs to the caller (the composer wires its own). Masked copy/cut: Gio broadcasts key events to every matching handler, so the kit cannot intercept Cmd+C/X — the masked field keeps its selection collapsed every frame (after Cmd+A, a double-click and a Shift+arrow drag) and the evidence asserts `WriteClipboard` is never issued on any of the three routes. `redact.Secret` is unreachable from the ui module (DA-E06), so the secret guarantee is structural — the kit logs nothing — and the test captures slog output across the whole interaction. Telex through IBus on Wayland and X11 stays the owner's eye check (DA-E02); on macOS, Simple Telex typed live in the running gallery commits "aa" to "â" in a kit field, and further typing commits into the TextArea in place through the same core. Kit v2. The shell's arrange popover (F10.20) and the create-workspace dialog (F10.14) use these. Step 0 (2026-11-18): the whole item estimates ~1,950 gross non-test lines, over the ~1,000 ceiling; Q-113 answered B (owner, 2026-11-18): one PR at that size, as Q-107 did for F10.16. Implemented on `feat/f10.18-kit-overlays` (rebased on main after F10.17a merged): measured 2,068 gross non-test Go lines (kit ~1,470, gallery ~500, roadmap/docs excluded). The floating-layer slots on `Env` are one menu, one popover and one dialog layer; a dialog over a dialog is refused with `ErrDialogAlreadyOpen`, and Escape belongs to the topmost layer (§7). Dialog Enter submits through the field core's `Submitted` event (the caller relays it to `Submit`), never a key reroute. Toasts run as a per-window manager on `Env`, drawing last through `FrameEnd`, with timers on the real frame delta and `Driver.WakeAt`, paused under the pointer. Evidence adds `TestNoDialogOnADialog`, `TestDialogFieldEnterSubmitsTheForm`, `TestToastsForOneObjectMerge`, UK-E04 `TestOverlayTextContrastOnSurfaceRaisedAndScrim`, UK-E07 `TestOverlayReducedMotionOpensAtOnceAndStaysQuiet`, UK-E10 `TestToastEventAreaIsClippedToItsBox`, UK-E11 `TestOverlayMotionSettlesAndRetargets`. `DialogWidth` is 420 dp (440 missed the fixed gallery window at UI 125% by 4 dp); nothing draws on the bare scrim, so UK-E04's scrim extension checks the components' text on their SurfaceRaised frames (4.5:1, both modes). Gio v0.10.3 facts the code relies on: key events go to the first matching `gtx.Event` caller, so `Env.TrackInput` stays the sole raw Tab reader and the dialog trap consumes the new `takeTab`/`takeShiftTab` flags; Gio clears the focus of a tag whose key handler is absent from a frame, so focus openers are real controls laid out every frame. |

The layout store contract is `host-api v0.16 §5.2` (base#22, owner option A, 2026-11-20): `layout` and `preferences` are versioned JSON the app defines (`{"schema_version": <positive integer>, ...}`); a `schema_version` higher than the app understands uses the default layout and does not overwrite the saved one until the user edits it; slots hold only object kind and ID. Design: `orchestration` (v0.2).

| F11.2 | Team proposal and decision (composite): `fugue_team_propose`, `team_proposal` item, approve, approve with edits, solo, reject, usage estimate, amend | `orchestration §5`, `transcript §3` | F11.1, F9.6 | OR-E13 (no task starts before approval; the edited model is the task's model), OR-E16 (amend needs the user) | Done | | Composite row (Q-106, owner, 2026-11-16): Done (F11.2a [#150](https://github.com/syurodev/fugue/pull/150) merged `a6305e6`; F11.2b [#159](https://github.com/syurodev/fugue/pull/159) merged `2cd2966`). Q-84 (settle tasks first): a team reaches `done`, `cancelled` or `failed` only after every task is terminal, so rejecting a proposal (`proposed` → `cancelled`) or cancelling a team first cancels each task (an `unknown` task through its cancel decision), then appends `TeamEnded`. Q-98–Q-101 (owner, 2026-11-16): approve makes the team `active` and queues its root tasks without starting anything (the start path is F11.3, the scheduler F11.6); the decision is stored on the proposal, the team and the `team_proposal` item and the proposing session is not woken in F11.2 (the "session receives the approved plan and the change list" part of OR-E13 moved to F11.3); `fugue_team_amend` is F11.2 and task retry is F11.3 (OR-E16 split); only Claude sessions receive the Fugue MCP server today, so F11.2 works and is tested with a Claude lead and F9.11 owns MCP for Codex and agy. |
| F11.2a | Team proposal: `fugue_team_propose` on the session principal, domain validation against the role catalog and the F11.1 rules, the §5.3 warnings, the `team_proposal` item and inbox card, the usage estimate, supersede, cancel on session end, `[session].teams` | `orchestration §5.1–5.3`, `transcript §3` | F11.1, F9.6 | `TestProposalValidatesAgainstTheRoleCatalog`, `TestOverlappingWriteScopeWithoutDependsOnWarns`, `TestUsageEstimateSaysNoDataInsteadOfGuessing`, `TestNoTaskStartsBeforeApproval`, `TestNewProposalSupersedesThePendingOne`, `TestPendingProposalIsCancelledWhenTheSessionEnds` | Done | [#150](https://github.com/syurodev/fugue/pull/150) | Merged `a6305e6`. No decision path in F11.2a: a pending proposal stays pending until F11.2b decides it. `[session].teams` defaults to true (Q-105) and is in `diffPolicy`; supersede invalidates the old inbox card first (Q-103). The inbox card is an interaction `question` (no new interaction kind), so the view and the proto carry an additive `body` field (interaction §8 extension needed by orchestration §5.3 card content; the `interaction` design page in base-doc must be updated to list `body`, proto field 14). The session's store task keys are namespaced under the proposal ID because `team_tasks.task_id` is globally unique. The session-end cancel runs on its own goroutine (endSession fires inside `requestClose` while the actor still writes; a direct append desyncs the actor's sequence) with the item flip best-effort through the actor's single-writer queue. Measured ~1,234 gross non-test lines (docs and generated code excluded), past the ~800 size stop; accepted by the owner as one PR (Q-108). Rebased on main after #144 (accounts) and #147 merged; #144 landed mid-flight, so the card's account line stays `unknown` in F11.2a and reading it from `AccountsService` moves to F11.2b. Review round 1 (owner): the size over the stop and the additive `body` field are **accepted**. Fixes: `Propose` and `CancelForSession` serialize per session under a keyed mutex (Q-103 holds when tool calls race), `Propose` re-checks token liveness under that lock before `CreateTeam` (a revoked token is visible before the session row closes, so the propose-versus-session-end race never leaves a pending proposal, Q-104), the item lookup goes by proposal ID through the new `idx_vendor_transcript_items_proposal` expression index (migration 0011, no scan-depth dependence) and `team_records` gained `idx_team_records_session_state`, and the session-end cancel goroutine runs on the host base context so shutdown stops it (fail-closed: a stopped cancel leaves the proposal pending) with the error code in its warning. Evidence adds `TestConcurrentProposalsLeaveOnePending`, `TestProposalRacingSessionEndNeverStaysPending`, `TestProposalStillFlipsAfterMoreThanAThousandItems`, `TestFailedItemFlipLeavesTheProposalFullyPending`. |
| F11.2b | Team decision: approve, approve with edits, solo, reject, auto-approve (at most 3 agents, Q-102), `fugue_team_amend` | `orchestration §5.3–5.4` | F11.2a | `TestEditedModelIsTheTaskModel`, `TestApproveQueuesRootTasksAndStartsNothing`, `TestSoloKeepsTheSessionSolo`, `TestSessionPrincipalCannotDecideAProposal`, `TestRejectSettlesTasksBeforeTeamEnded`, `TestAutoApproveOnlyForCatalogReadRolesUnderTheLimit`, `TestAmendNeedsTheUserForANewRoleOrMoreAgents` | Done | [#159](https://github.com/syurodev/fugue/pull/159) | Merged `2cd2966`. Only a local principal decides; a session principal can never approve, edit, choose solo or reject (Q-99: no wake before F11.3). OR-E16's retry half stays with F11.3. Q-111 decided A: auto-approve is `[session].teams_auto_approve`, default false, in `diffPolicy`. The approve-with-edits document is the typed `teams.EditSet` (versioned, strict decode, never logged) carried in the answer's free-text field; F11.7's UI builds against it. Deviation (accepted 2026-11-17): an account-per-role edit is refused with a typed error — stage A assigns no accounts to roles. The amend insertion store method is transactional with the decision/amend event (`DecideTeam`/`AmendTeam`) so an approved amend can never be recorded without its queued tasks; added tasks are never started (start path is F11.3, Q-98). The lead's amend notice is the tool result only — no transcript item, no wake (Q-99). ### After stage A

Stage B list (activation §7, v0.14): the native harness (every `Deferred` row above), OpenRouter
and other API providers, Sign in with ChatGPT, GLM through Claude Code (VR-E12), account
failover, code reading, web, MCP and plugins, attachments, observability, Windows, packaging and
auto-update, other users, remote access. Also: import of DevKit data and the DevKit modules that
Fugue may rebuild (git, tasks, terminal, database, ssh, API client…). Each area needs its own
design and plan before it enters this board.

## 5. Proposed layout

Input to ADR F0.2, which is approved with the deviations it lists; where this sketch and
[ADR 0001](adr/0001-package-layout.md) differ, the ADR is authoritative.

```text
cmd/fugue/                 main: fugue serve and subcommands
internal/
  platform/                clock, ids, supervise, redact, errcode (stdlib only)
  config/                  typed configuration; the only reader of environment variables
  kernel/                  pure decide/evolve, commands, events, invariants (stdlib only, imports nothing of this module)
  codec/                   canonical codec v1
  crypto/  vault/          ported from DevKit
  storage/                 DB open, pragmas, migrations (DDL owner), data-directory lock, exec_host
  execstore/               SQL for the other exec_* tables, protected payloads
  coordinator/             per-session actor, dispatch, timers, scheduler
  contextengine/
  modeladapter/            neutral contract; anthropic/, openai/, faux/
  credentials/
  tools/                   registry, descriptors; concrete tools in subpackages
  interaction/  sandbox/  snapshot/  workspacememory/
  app/                     composition root and application services
  hostapi/                 Connect handlers; generated code in hostapi/internal/pb, importable only here
  arch/                    architecture tests
proto/fugue/<module>/v1/
tools/fuguevet/
quality/                   coverage and ratchet baselines
docs/                      roadmap, quality-gates, dependencies, ports, adr/
```

Dependency direction: `platform` ← `kernel` ← `coordinator` ← `app` ← `hostapi` ← `cmd`.
The vendor domain (`vendor`, `vendorstore`, `transcript`, `teams`) and the `ui/` module are laid
out by ADR 0002 (F9.1).
`execstore`, `modeladapter` and `tools` implement ports defined by `coordinator` and are wired
together only in `app`.

| Q-48 | 2026-11-10 | F9.5b | Steer message arriving after turn result: if a steer message reaches stdin after the active turn has concluded, what should the adapter do? If a subsequent Send message is absorbed into the vendor turn opened by a late steer, it is detected via registered send UUID, emits a notice (`absorbed_into_vendor_turn`), and attributes the turn completion/result back to the Fugue turn. | Q-84 | 2026-11-14 | F11.1 | May a task event be appended to a team whose state is terminal (`cancelled`/`done`/`failed`)? The store's task branch validates only against the task row, so a cancelled team can record a task that advances after cancellation; rejecting every task event on a terminal team could also block the settlement events that cancel or end a team's tasks (F11.2/F11.3 semantics). Which lifecycle rule should the store enforce, if any? | settle tasks first (decided by the owner, 2026-11-14): the store accepts a team event that moves the team to done, cancelled or failed only when every task of that team is terminal (done, failed, stopped, cancelled; unknown is NOT terminal); the check runs inside the same transaction as the append, otherwise a typed error is returned and the store is unchanged. Once the team is terminal, every task event for that team is rejected with a typed error and nothing is appended. Cancellation order (F11.2/F11.3): cancel each task first (an unknown task goes through its cancel decision), then append TeamEnded. Evidence: `TestTerminalTeamRequiresSettledTasks`, `TestTaskEventOnTerminalTeamIsRejected` |

| Q-109 | 2026-11-17 | F0.10 | CI is slow and flaky (last 60 PR runs: 36 succeeded, 11 failed, 13 cancelled; the nightly was red 2026-11-12 to 2026-11-16, every time on `repeat-tests`). Measured: `go test -race -shuffle=on -count=1 ./...` is 7 min 41 s wall (12 CPU threads) and the sum of package times is 1,089 s; `internal/app` 209 s, `internal/vendorstore` 149 s, `internal/execstore` 101 s, `internal/secrets` 74 s, `internal/arch` 72 s, `internal/hostapi` 59 s. Seven tests are above 45 s: `TestTranscriptPageOver10000ItemsIsStableWithoutDuplicatesOrGaps` 121 s, `TestAppend10000PayloadsKeepsTotalConsistent` 100 s, `TestFindSparseAllocationBound` 67 s, `internal/arch` `TestTableOwnership/repository` 66 s, `TestOversizedContentAndRawAreStoredHeadAndTailWithFlags` 55 s, `TestRatchets/repository` 51 s, `TestVerificationEvidence/repo` 45 s; 13 tests are above 30 s. A CPU profile of the first shows 45% of its time in sqlite statement preparation (`appendRows` prepares every statement for every row) and 29% in `checkptr` under `-race`. The nightly failures: all five runs ended with `internal/app` hitting the 10-minute package panic (one also `internal/vendorstore`, one also failed `internal/coordinator` `TestSessionEdgeCases` and `TestToolObservationFencing`). After this change, same machine and one run (the machine is shared, so treat it as indicative): sum of package times 765 s, `internal/app` 94 s, `internal/vendorstore` 91 s, five tests above 30 s (`TestFindSparseAllocationBound` 57 s, `internal/arch` `TestTableOwnership/repository` 55 s, `TestFugueNeverAnswersAVendorRequestInAnyMode` 43 s, `TestRatchets/repository` 39 s, `TestVerificationEvidence/repo` 34 s). Test waits: 22 `runtime.Gosched` calls in test loops and no `time.Sleep` polling; 20 of them waited with no deadline (`for {}` or `for range 100000`; `t.Context()` ends only when the test ends, so `waitForFile`, `waitPidGone` and `waitForRunningState` also spun to the package timeout): `internal/app` accounts_service_test.go:262, interaction_ordering_test.go:173, 186, 214, transcript_test.go:121, 146, 152, vendor_sessions_test.go:745; `internal/process` containment_test.go:138, 173, fakevendor_unix_test.go:283, stop_test.go:454, 630, signal_darwin_test.go:122; `internal/transcript` delta_test.go:45; `internal/vendoradapter` agy session_test.go:608, claude session_control_test.go:59, session_test.go:47, 516, codex session_test.go:496. The two that are bounded work stay: `internal/app` host_grace_test.go:109 and `internal/vendoradapter/claude` session_test.go:609. Proposal for F0.10 (about 330 gross non-test lines, under the 800 limit, so one PR): (1) `boundedwait` analyzer plus `testutil.Spin` and `testutil.WaitFor`, and every violation above fixed with the hang timeout; (2) `fuguegate slowtests -json <file> -max <dur>` and a report-only 30 s step in the PR `test` job, documented in quality-gates §6; (3) shrink the PR variant of the two heaviest tests (637 items and 500 appends) and run the 10,000 variants in the nightly `perf` job. Follow-ups, not in this PR: (a) split `internal/app` by owner so no package needs more than half the 10-minute timeout (it is 209 s for one count here and 200 to 390 s per count in CI); (b) prepare the vendorstore statements once per transaction, a production change worth about half of the 10,000-item test; (c) four `internal/arch` tests take 24 to 66 s each (186 s together); (d) the sparse-allocation and oversized-content tests (67 s and 55 s) need a smaller or perf-tag variant; (e) make the budget a required failure once the list above 30 s is empty. #150 must replace its `awaitCancelledProposal` loop with `testutil.Spin` or `Await` before it merges after this PR; no allowlist entry was added | Proposed; owner decision pending |

(2) `claude auth login` (2.1.319) prints an OAuth URL and then waits for the user to paste a code into the login process's stdin ("Paste code here if prompted >"); the settled StartLogin/CancelLogin shape has no input channel, so a Fugue-started claude login can never complete without one. Which for agy, and B1 or B2 for claude? | A decided by the owner (2026-11-18): agy answers with a typed Unimplemented/FailedPrecondition error ("agy has no login command; sign in from the agy app"); claude is **B2** — F9.10b runs `claude auth login` (the default claude.ai flow), relays the printed URL verbatim and relies on the browser's localhost callback, and when the CLI prints its paste prompt and the login has not completed when the timeout fires it ends with the typed outcome `needs_terminal` (a paste relay, if ever wanted, belongs with the F10.15 secret-answer mechanism); codex is `--device-auth` only and the default browser flow is never run again, not even in tests or measurements; timeout 15 minutes. Evidence adds `TestAgyStartLoginIsUnsupported` and `TestClaudeLoginWithoutCallbackEndsNeedsTerminal`. |

| Q-116 | 2026-11-18 | F11.3 | Which queued tasks start on their own, and what does `fugue_task_start` do? `orchestration §5.3` ("Fugue starts the tasks without dependencies") and §6.4 ("Fugue starts when the dependencies are done") say Fugue starts plan tasks; OR-E02 and §6.6 give the delegator `fugue_task_start`. A (recommended): the dispatcher starts every task of the approved plan whose parent task is running and whose `depends_on` are all `done`; a task inserted by `fugue_team_amend` (F11.2b leaves it `queued`) starts only through its delegator's `fugue_task_start(task_id)`, which is idempotent and returns at once; the first wave is therefore the lead's direct children without dependencies. B: nothing auto-starts except dependents; every first-wave task needs `fugue_task_start`. Which? | A decided by the owner, 2026-11-18. |
| Q-117 | 2026-11-18 | F11.3 | The lead root task is a task row, but its agent is the session that proposed. A (recommended): the start path binds the proposing session to the lead task (`TaskAssigned` with that session, then `TaskStarted`), spawns nothing and creates no worktree; the lead session keeps its workspace cwd. Tool roles come from the store (session → task → hierarchy role, `ActiveTeamForSession`), never from the token role, which is fixed at spawn as `assistant` (a solo session becomes lead without a respawn); a task session's token role is minted from its task so OR-E10 (a member's token cannot open a lead tool) is enforced in Go. B: respawn the lead as a new session. Which? | A decided by the owner, 2026-11-18. |
| Q-118 | 2026-11-18 | F11.3, F11.6 | Where does the F11.3 start path end and the F11.6 scheduler begin? A (recommended): F11.3 owns a dispatcher (event-driven after a decision, a task end and an unlock; creation order; restart never re-runs a task) behind an `Admitter` port whose F11.3 implementation admits while `VendorSessions` has capacity (`waiting/capacity` leaves the task `queued`) and while a delegator has fewer than 4 running direct children (the §11 figure as a fixed safety cap). F11.6 replaces it with the three slot tiers, the 85% quota gate, account choice, the budgets, the watchdog and recovery decisions; F11.3 builds no slot accounting, quota read, deadline, silence timer or priority order. B: no cap in F11.3 beyond VendorSessions capacity. Which? | A decided by the owner, 2026-11-18. |
| Q-119 | 2026-11-18 | F11.3, F11.4 | Worktree and cwd for a task session. (1) `SelectSessionCwd` rejects the data directory and descendants (Q-57) and gitops worktrees live at `<data>/worktrees/<repo>/<task>`, so the start path cannot use it. A (recommended): the cwd is host-derived from `gitops.TaskWorktreePath` for the task and the session's recorded workspace root (never client input), and only a path under that exact worktrees tree is accepted. (2) The repository is the proposing session's `WorkspaceRoot`; the team branch is created at the first task start from that root's current HEAD (`CreateTeamBranch`), a member branches from its manager's branch, a manager from the team branch. A root that is not a git repository fails the task closed as `failed(worktree)` with a typed code and a notice to the delegator; nothing runs without a worktree. B: allow task sessions in the workspace root. Which? | A decided by the owner, 2026-11-18. |

A (recommended): a `read` role starts with Fugue's `plan` approval mode, a `write` role with `ask`; never allow-always for a task session; every approval request is relayed verbatim to the user's inbox and never to a mailbox (OR-E14). The role's `checks` are not run in F11.3 (F11.5). B: add a per-role approval mode to the catalog. Which? | A decided by the owner, 2026-11-18. |
| Q-121 | 2026-11-18 | F11.3 | §6.1 gives a mailbox only to leads and managers, but `fugue_task_ask` and `fugue_task_send` do not block, so a member that asked must be woken with the answer. A (recommended): the one table serves every task session: leads and managers get the batched digest, a member gets an answer or a `fugue_task_send` message at once (no 30 s wait), each message to its recipient task's session. B: members poll `fugue_task_status` for the answer (violates OR-03). Which? | A decided by the owner, 2026-11-18. |
| Q-122 | 2026-11-18 | F11.3 | Mailbox persistence and the at-least-once rule (§6.1). A (recommended): new table `team_mailbox_messages` (reserved for F11.3 by ADR 0002; migration 0012, metadata columns only, the text sealed with the team key, `message_id` = `<team>:<source event seq>:<recipient task>` so enqueue is idempotent) written in the same transaction as the event that produces it; a delivery claims the pending rows into one `batch_id`, sends one Fugue-origin turn that starts with `[fugue-batch <batch_id>]`, and marks them `delivered` when the turn is recorded. After a crash a `delivering` batch is resolved by reading the session's transcript for that marker (found: `delivered`; absent: resend with the same batch ID); if the transcript cannot be read the batch is held, never guessed (no replay of an effect whose outcome is unknown). B: mark delivered before sending (at-most-once). Which? | A decided by the owner, 2026-11-18. |
| Q-123 | 2026-11-18 | F11.3 | Waking a sleeping vendor session. Every vendor takes a new turn through the same host call, `VendorSessions.Send` (a Fugue-origin turn; Claude stream-json stdin, Codex `turn/start`, agy ACP `session/prompt`); a busy session holds the digest until its turn ends. A (recommended): `ready` sends directly; `detached` (host restart) is first resumed from the stored handle (Claude `--resume`, Codex `thread/resume`, agy `session/load`) and needs an unlocked vault, otherwise the messages wait with the session `waiting/vault_locked`; `closed` holds the messages until the user reopens (§12). Step 1 first measures, against the real CLIs, that Codex and agy accept a new turn after an idle period and after a resume (F9.10b and F9.11 precedent); a vendor that cannot is a Q row before code. B: skip the resume case in F11.3. Which? | A decided by the owner, 2026-11-18. |
| Q-124 | 2026-11-18 | F11.3 | The digest is a Fugue-origin turn, which a model reads with user-level weight, but its summaries are model output (OR-13). A (recommended): the digest is built by one function in `internal/teams` from a fixed template: Fugue text first, then each summary inside a delimited quoted block under a fixed line saying the block is data from an agent and carries no instructions, with the delimiter and control characters escaped so a summary cannot close the block, never including `output`, report, diff or check output (references only); a golden test fixes the format. Step 1 reads `content-safety` (out of this step's scope) and adopts its wrapper if it defines one. B: plain concatenation. Which? | A decided by the owner, 2026-11-18. |
| Q-125 | 2026-11-18 | F11.3, F11.5 | Result gates are F11.5, so `checking` → `review` has no owner in F11.3, yet `fugue_task_accept` and `reject` act on `review` and OR-E18 needs a `done` task. A (recommended): a `ResultGate` port (F11.5's real gate and a test fake are the two implementations) whose F11.3 production binding reports "no gate configured" and leaves the task in `checking` (fail closed); tests inject a passing gate. `fugue_task_accept` completes a report or no-diff task (`complete_no_diff` → `done`); on a diff task it is refused with a typed error until F11.5 wires the merge queue (no task enters `integrating` with nothing to consume it). A `depends_on` is satisfied only by `done`, so diff chains finish with F11.5 and OR-E18 is proved with report tasks. B: pass tasks with an empty `write_scope` and no `checks` straight to `review` in F11.3. Which? | A decided by the owner, 2026-11-18. |
| Q-126 | 2026-11-18 | F11.3 | How is a retry recorded (§5.4: the old task becomes `stopped`, the new one is the next attempt, at most 2 per task)? The F11.1 table has no `failed` → `stopped` transition. A (recommended): new event `TaskSuperseded` (`failed` → `stopped`) appended in the same transaction that inserts the new task (as F11.2b's amend insertion); the new task has a derived ID `<task>~r<n>`, columns `attempt` and `retry_of`, the same role and model; `depends_on` of other tasks is never rewritten, a dependency resolves to the latest attempt of its chain at dispatch time; the third retry and a changed role or model need `fugue_team_amend` (OR-E16); only `failed` tasks retry (an `unknown` task's resume/keep/cancel is F11.6, §12). `TestTaskTransitionTableCoversEveryStateAndEvent` is extended. B: keep the old task `failed` and only link the new one (deviates from §3.1). Which? | A decided by the owner, 2026-11-18. |
| Q-127 | 2026-11-18 | F11.3 | Where do a task's report, output and diff live (§4, §6.3)? `team_tasks` stores only the sealed spec; gitops has no diff read. A (recommended): `fugue_task_done` stores the sealed `summary` (at most 1 KiB, longer is cut with a flag) and `output` (at most 64 KiB, rejected over it) in the `TaskReported` payload; the `report` part is the same text (no file path from an agent is ever opened); for a `diff` or `both` task `fugue_task_done` first runs `gitops.CommitWorktree` so the branch holds the work, and the `diff` part is a bounded, paged read-only `Manager.DiffAgainstParent` added to gitops (~80 lines); `checks` returns the F11.5 result once it exists and `not_run` before. B: `diff` returns `unavailable` until F11.5. Which? | A decided by the owner, 2026-11-18. |
| Q-128 | 2026-11-18 | F11.3 | OR-E03 validates `output` against the task's `output_schema`, and `go.mod` has no JSON Schema library. A (recommended): add `github.com/santhosh-tekuri/jsonschema/v6` (Apache-2.0, pure Go, no reflection of our types) with a row in `docs/dependencies.md`, used only in `internal/teams`; a wrong or uncompilable schema keeps the result with `schema_valid = false` and the error text (bounded, no output content). B: a hand-written validator for the type, required, enum and items subset (no new module, narrower than "JSON Schema"). Which? | A decided by the owner, 2026-11-18. |
| Q-129 | 2026-11-18 | F11.3 | Forwarding a question (§6.2: the delegator answers, passes it up one level, or passes it to the user) has no tool in the §6.6 table. A (recommended): `fugue_task_answer(task_id, answer?, forward_to?)` where `forward_to` is `parent` or `user`; a forward creates the manager's own `fugue_task_ask` (its task goes `asking`, the original stays `asking`), and the answer that returns is delivered to the original asker by the same mailbox; a manager's forward goes only to its own delegator, so a lead never sees a member's question unforwarded (OR-E14). B: no forward; the delegator asks its own question by hand. Which? | A decided by the owner, 2026-11-18. |
| Q-130 | 2026-11-18 | F11.3 | Team notes and brief (§6.5). A (recommended): the brief is the proposal `brief` already sealed in the `TeamProposed` payload (16 KiB); a note is a new event `TeamNoted` (`active` → `active`, sealed payload carrying the author task ID, at most 2 KiB, at most 100 notes per team), written only by a lead or manager; `fugue_team_brief(offset?, limit?)` returns brief then notes in order, at most 16 KiB per call. B: notes in a table outside the event log. Which? | A decided by the owner, 2026-11-18. |

B: also fail the task on a failed or interrupted turn. Which? | A decided by the owner, 2026-11-18. |

B: keep relayed tickets open for the session lifetime until answered or session end. Which? |  A decided by the owner, 2026-11-20: fixed in a separate bug PR right after F10.5b. |

| 2026-11-17 | F11.2a | Fixed the session-end cancel race the #150 stress job caught (`TestProposalStillFlipsAfterMoreThanAThousandItems` hung: team cancelled, item still pending). Cause: the cancel goroutine journals the item flip through the session actor, but the actor could finish closing first and the store refuses writes to a closed session (`failed_precondition`, reproduced locally; the `unavailable` warnings in CI were the second `endSession` call at actor exit hitting a cancelled context or closed store at teardown). Fix: the actor holds its final close writes until the in-flight cancel settles (`SessionEndSettled`, still serving commands), `CancelForSession` flips the item before cancelling the team and a failed flip leaves the proposal fully pending (transient `unavailable` retried up to 3 times), supersede already flips first, shutdown-time cancel errors no longer warn, and the test polls are bounded by `testutil.Poll`. Evidence adds `TestFailedItemFlipLeavesTheProposalFullyPending`. | Agent |

Review findings: (1) the busy ring turned about the origin of its box, not its center (`Spinner.Layout` rotated about `f32.Point{}`), so it swung off its slot — fixed, `TestButtonBusySpinnerSitsInItsSlot` reads the ink at pixel scale 1 and 1.25 (it fails on the old rotation); (2) UK-E09 proved nothing because `windowSize(px, ui)` multiplied the window by the UI scale — one fixed 1360×1000 dp window per pixel scale now, pages flow their blocks and wrap, and `checkPageBounds` also rejects control-vs-control (focus rings included), control-vs-text and page-column overflow; (3) the `eglInitialize failed: 0x3001` flake was reproduced on Linux under Xvfb with Mesa software GL in a container — every run once a process opened a second headless window: Gio ends a window with `eglTerminate` on the process-wide default display, and the next `eglInitialize` fails there (the earlier per-test mutex never helped, the windows were already sequential) — every headless window now comes from `ui/internal/glwin.Open` and is never released, so no process ever re-initializes the terminated display, and repeated runs of the kit, theme, glwin and gallery packages under Xvfb pass (a shared cache behind a mutex was tried first and dropped: it needed two `nolint` directives, which the ratchet refuses, and the cause was never concurrency); no workflow or gate file touched; (4) busy and disabled controls paint no hover or press wash (`TestBusyShowsNoHoverOrPressWash`). Found on the way: the hover and press followers were zero-value `motion.Follower`s with no spring, so after the first frame a real hover never changed (the gallery only showed it because the preview forces it on frame one) — built with `NewFollower`, `TestButtonMotionSettlesAndRetargets` pins it; and Gio draws into an sRGB framebuffer and blends in linear light, so a translucent Fg wash on a dark surface lifts far more than its alpha says (Fg at 6% over AppBg renders as `0x44`, not `0x21`): the UK-E04 test composited in sRGB arithmetic and passed what the screen failed — it now blends in linear light and the control stacks changed to match (press replaces the selected wash, which replaces hover; the ripple fades once half spread). Review of the first shots then showed the spec's alphas are perceived targets: the dark fills and borders were far too strong. Every wash and fill token in `theme` is now solved to its perceived value (`perceived` in `wash.go`; alpha starts at 64/255 for 8-bit precision and rises where a deep tint would leave the gamut), pinned by `TestInteractionWashesDeriveFromFg` (math) and `TestWashTokensRenderTheirPerceivedValue` (headless pixels, ±2 per channel), with UK-E04 still on the linear blend; the danger variant got its own hover and press tints instead of stacking, the ripple is a faint perceived-11% token over the whole-body pressed fill, and the gallery's forced pressed preview shows no ripple (real presses do, slowly with S). The focus ring takes the control's own tone (owner decision, base#16, ui-kit §3.8): `Action` for primary, solid `Status.Danger` for danger, `FocusBorder` otherwise, always solid, 2 dp wide and 2 dp outside; every variant's ring measures at least 3:1 against all five chrome surfaces in both modes (`TestFocusRingReaches3to1InEveryVariant`).
